478.         private async void ScheduleAdFromScte35Payload(TimedMetadataTrack timedMetadataTrack, uint presentation_time_delta, uint timescale, uint event_duration, string scte35payload)
479.         {
480.             // TODO: Parse SCTE-35
481.             //
482.             // Ref:
483.             // http://www.scte.org/SCTEDocs/Standards/ANSI_SCTE%20214-3%202015.pdf
484.             //
485.             // Eg:
486.             //
487.             // <SpliceInfoSection ptsAdjustment="0" scte35:tier="4095">
488.             //  <SpliceInsert spliceEventId="147467497"
489.             //                spliceEventCancelIndicator="false"
490.             //                outOfNetworkIndicator="false"
491.             //                uniqueProgramId="0"
492.             //                availNum="0"
493.             //                availsExpected="0"
494.             //                spliceImmediateFlag="false" >
495.             //    <Program>
496.             //      <SpliceTime ptsTime="6257853600"/>
497.             //    </Program>
498.             //    <BreakDuration autoReturn="true" duration="900000"/>
499.             //  </SpliceInsert>
500.             // </SpliceInfoSection>
501.             //
502.             //
503. 
504.             // We use the metadata track object which fired the Cue to walk our way back up the
505.             // media object stack to find our original AdaptiveMediaSource.
506.             //
507.             // The AdaptiveMediaSource is required because the SCTE-35 payload includes
508.             // timing offsets which are relative to the original content PTS -- see below.
509.             var ams = timedMetadataTrack.PlaybackItem.Source.AdaptiveMediaSource;
510. 
511.             if (ams != null && timescale != 0)
512.             {
513.                 // ++++
514.                 // NOTE: DO NOT PARSE SCTE35 LIKE THIS IN PRODUCTION CODE!
515.                 //
516.                 // Reminder:
517.                 //
518.                 // THIS CODE IS PROVIDED *AS IS* WITHOUT WARRANTY OF
519.                 // ANY KIND, EITHER EXPRESS OR IMPLIED, INCLUDING ANY
520.                 // IMPLIED WARRANTIES OF FITNESS FOR A PARTICULAR
521.                 // PURPOSE, MERCHANTABILITY, OR NON-INFRINGEMENT.
522.                 //
523.                 // We will not demonstrate proper SCTE35 parsing in this sample,
524.                 // but instead we search through the xml find the ptsTime if present,
525.                 // and use it to schedule an ad; keeping in place the various time offsets.
526.                 //
527.                 // e.g.: var sampleScte35Payload = "<SpliceInfoSection ptsAdjustment=\"0\" scte35:tier=\"4095\">< SpliceInsert spliceEventId = \"147467497\" spliceEventCancelIndicator = \"false\" outOfNetworkIndicator = \"false\" uniqueProgramId = \"0\" availNum = \"0\" availsExpected = \"0\" spliceImmediateFlag = \"false\" > < Program >< SpliceTime ptsTime = \"6257853600\" /></ Program >  < BreakDuration autoReturn = \"true\" duration = \"900000\" />  </ SpliceInsert > </ SpliceInfoSection > ";
528.                 //
529.                 var xmlStrings = scte35payload.Split(new string[] { "<", "/", ">", " ", "=", "\"" }, StringSplitOptions.RemoveEmptyEntries);
530.                 string ptsTime = string.Empty;
531.                 for (int i = 0; i < xmlStrings.Length; i++)
532.                 {
533.                     if (xmlStrings[i] == "ptsTime")
534.                     {
535.                         if (i + 1 < xmlStrings.Length)
536.                         {
537.                             ptsTime = xmlStrings[i + 1];
538.                             break;
539.                         }

177.         private void CreateMediaBreaksForItem(MediaPlaybackItem item)
178.         {
179.             if (item != null)
180.             {
181.                 // We have two ads that will be repeated.
182.                 var redSkyUri = new Uri("http://az29176.vo.msecnd.net/videocontent/RedSky_FoxRiverWisc_GettyImagesRF-499617760_1080_HD_EN-US.mp4");
183.                 var flowersUri = new Uri("http://az29176.vo.msecnd.net/videocontent/CrocusTL_FramePoolRM_688-580-676_1080_HD_EN-US.mp4");
184. 
185.                 // One option is to create a separate MediaPlaybackItem for each of your ads.
186.                 // You might choose to do this if each ad needs different reporting information.
187.                 // Another option is to re-use MediaPlaybackItems in different MediaBreaks.
188.                 // This scenario demonstrates both patterns.
189.                 var ad1 = new MediaPlaybackItem(MediaSource.CreateFromUri(redSkyUri));
190.                 ad1.Source.CustomProperties["contentId"] = "Ad1_ID";
191.                 ad1.Source.CustomProperties["description"] = "Red Sky";

197.                 ad2.Source.CustomProperties["contentId"] = "Ad2_ID";
198.                 ad2.Source.CustomProperties["description"] = "Flowers";
199.                 ad2.Source.CustomProperties["uri"] = flowersUri.ToString();
200.                 RegisterForMediaSourceEvents(ad2.Source);
201.                 RegisterForMediaPlaybackItemEvents(ad2);
202. 
203.                 var ad3 = new MediaPlaybackItem(MediaSource.CreateFromUri(redSkyUri));
204.                 ad3.Source.CustomProperties["contentId"] = "Ad3_ID";
205.                 ad3.Source.CustomProperties["description"] = "Red Sky 2";
206.                 ad3.Source.CustomProperties["uri"] = redSkyUri.ToString();
207.                 RegisterForMediaSourceEvents(ad3.Source);
208.                 RegisterForMediaPlaybackItemEvents(ad3);
209. 
210.                 // Create a PrerollBreak on your main content.
211.                 if (item.BreakSchedule.PrerollBreak == null)
212.                 {
213.                     item.BreakSchedule.PrerollBreak = new MediaBreak(MediaBreakInsertionMethod.Interrupt);
214.                 }
215. 
216.                 // Add the ads to the PrerollBreak in the order you want them played
217.                 item.BreakSchedule.PrerollBreak.PlaybackList.Items.Add(ad1);
218.                 item.BreakSchedule.PrerollBreak.PlaybackList.Items.Add(ad2);
219. 
220.                 // Add the ads to the MidRoll break at 10% into the main content.
221.                 // To do this, we need to wait until the main MediaPlaybackItem is fully loaded by the player
222.                 // so that we know its Duration. This will happen on MediaSource.OpenOperationCompleted.
223.                 item.Source.OpenOperationCompleted += (sender, args) =>
224.                 {
225.                     var attachedItem = MediaPlaybackItem.FindFromMediaSource(sender);
226.                     if (sender.Duration.HasValue)
227.                     {
228.                         // For live streaming, the duration will be TimeSpan.MaxValue, which won't work for this scenario,
229.                         // so we'll assume the total duration is 2 minutes for the purpose of ad insertion.
230.                         bool isLiveMediaSource = item.Source.AdaptiveMediaSource != null ? item.Source.AdaptiveMediaSource.IsLive : false;
--- RESULT ---
170.             }
171. 
172.             // We use a helper class that logs all the events for the MediaSource:
173.             mediaSourceLogger = new MediaSourceLogger(LoggerControl, source);
174. 
175.             // Save the original Uri.
176.             source.CustomProperties["uri"] = uri.ToString();
177. 
178.             // You're likely to put a content tracking id into the CustomProperties.
179.             source.CustomProperties["contentId"] = Guid.NewGuid().ToString();
180. 
181.             var mpItem = new MediaPlaybackItem(source);
182. 
183.             // We use a helper class that logs all the events for the MediaPlaybackItem:
184.             mediaPlaybackItemLogger = new MediaPlaybackItemLogger(LoggerControl, mpItem);
185. 
186.             HideDescriptionOnSmallScreen();
187. 
188.             return mpItem;
189.         }

205.         private void InitializeBitrateLists(AdaptiveMediaSource aMS)
206.         {
207.             var sortedBitrates = aMS.AvailableBitrates.OrderByDescending(br => br).Select(br => new BitrateItem(br)).ToArray();
208.             InitialBitrateList.ItemsSource = sortedBitrates;
209.             var selected = sortedBitrates.First(item => item.Bitrate == aMS.InitialBitrate);
210.             InitialBitrateList.SelectedItem = sortedBitrates.FirstOrDefault(item => item.Bitrate == aMS.InitialBitrate);
211. 
212.             var nullableSortedBitrates = (new BitrateItem[] { new BitrateItem(null) }).Concat(sortedBitrates).ToArray();
213.             DesiredMaxBitrateList.ItemsSource = DesiredMinBitrateList.ItemsSource = nullableSortedBitrates;
214. 
215.             DesiredMaxBitrateList.SelectedItem = nullableSortedBitrates.First(item => item.Bitrate == aMS.DesiredMaxBitrate);
216.             DesiredMinBitrateList.SelectedItem = nullableSortedBitrates.First(item => item.Bitrate == aMS.DesiredMinBitrate);
217.         }
218. 
219.         // An argument exception will be thrown if the following constraint is not met:
220.         //   DesiredMinBitrate <= InitialBitrate <= DesiredMaxBitrate
221.         private bool IsValidBitrateCombination(uint? desiredMinBitrate, uint? desiredMaxBitrate, uint initialBitrate)
222.         {
223.             // The ">" operator returns false if either operand is null. We take advantage of this
224.             // by testing in this manner. Do NOT "optimize" this by reversing the sense of the test
225.             // to "return desiredMinBitrate <= initialBitrate && initialBitrate <= desiredMaxBitrate;"
226.             if (desiredMinBitrate > initialBitrate || initialBitrate > desiredMaxBitrate)
227.             {
228.                 return false;
229.             }
230.             return true;
231.         }
232. 
233.         private void InitialBitrateList_SelectionChanged(object sender, SelectionChangedEventArgs e)
234.         {
235.             var selection = (e.AddedItems.Count > 0) ? (BitrateItem)e.AddedItems[0] : null;
236.             if (selection != null && adaptiveMediaSource.InitialBitrate != selection.Bitrate)
237.             {
238.                 if (IsValidBitrateCombination(adaptiveMediaSource.DesiredMinBitrate, adaptiveMediaSource.DesiredMaxBitrate, selection.Bitrate.Value))
239.                 {
240.                     adaptiveMediaSource.InitialBitrate = selection.Bitrate.Value;
241.                 }
242.                 else
243.                 {
244.                     InitialBitrateList.SelectedItem = e.RemovedItems[0];
245.                 }
246.             }
247.         }
248. 
249.         private void DesiredMinBitrateList_SelectionChanged(object sender, SelectionChangedEventArgs e)
250.         {
251.             var selection = (e.AddedItems.Count > 0) ? (BitrateItem)e.AddedItems[0] : null;
252.             if (selection != null && adaptiveMediaSource.DesiredMinBitrate != selection.Bitrate)
253.             {
254.                 if (IsValidBitrateCombination(selection.Bitrate, adaptiveMediaSource.DesiredMaxBitrate, adaptiveMediaSource.InitialBitrate))
255.                 {
256.                     adaptiveMediaSource.DesiredMinBitrate = selection.Bitrate;
257.                 }
258.                 else
259.                 {
260.                     DesiredMinBitrateList.SelectedItem = e.RemovedItems[0];
261.                 }
262.             }
263.         }
264. 
265.         private void DesiredMaxBitrateList_SelectionChanged(object sender, SelectionChangedEventArgs e)
266.         {
267.             var selection = (e.AddedItems.Count > 0) ? (BitrateItem)e.AddedItems[0] : null;
268.             if (selection != null && adaptiveMediaSource.DesiredMaxBitrate != selection.Bitrate)
269.             {
270.                 if (IsValidBitrateCombination(adaptiveMediaSource.DesiredMinBitrate, selection.Bitrate, adaptiveMediaSource.InitialBitrate))
271.                 {
272.                     adaptiveMediaSource.DesiredMaxBitrate = selection.Bitrate;
273.                 }
274.                 else
275.                 {
276.                     DesiredMaxBitrateList.SelectedItem = e.RemovedItems[0];
277.                 }
278.             }
279.         }

205.         {

207.                 return;

213.                 DesiredSeekableWindowSize.Text = adaptiveMediaSource.DesiredSeekableWindowSize.HasValue ? $"{adaptiveMediaSource.DesiredSeekableWindowSize.Value.ToString("c")}" : "";
214.                 DesiredLiveOffset.Text = $"{adaptiveMediaSource.DesiredLiveOffset.ToString("c")}";
215.                 MinLiveOffset.Text = adaptiveMediaSource.MinLiveOffset.HasValue ? $"{adaptiveMediaSource.MinLiveOffset.Value.ToString("c")}" : "";
216.             });
217.         }

260.         private void UpdateSeekableRangesControl(IReadOnlyList<MediaTimeRange> SeekableRanges)
261.         {
262.             if (SeekableRanges.Count > 0)
263.             {
264.                 var SeekableRangeMin = SeekableRanges.First();
265.                 m_PlaybackPositionMinTime = SeekableRangeMin.Start;
266. 
267.                 var SeekableRangeMax = SeekableRanges.Last();
268.                 m_PlaybackPositionMaxTime = SeekableRangeMax.End;
269.             }
270.             else
271.             {
272.                 m_PlaybackPositionMaxTime = TimeSpan.FromHours(1);
273.                 m_PlaybackPositionMinTime = TimeSpan.Zero;
274.             }
275. 
276.             // TODO[migrate-018]: see PATTERNS.md#threading
277.             var task = Dispatcher.RunAsync(CoreDispatcherPriority.Normal, () =>
278.             {
279.                 StartPosition.Text = m_PlaybackPositionMinTime.ToString("c");
280.                 EndPosition.Text = m_PlaybackPositionMaxTime.ToString("c");
281.             });
282. 
283.             string ranges = "";
284.             foreach (var range in SeekableRanges)
285.             {
286.                 ranges += $"[{range.Start},{range.End}],";
287.             }
288.             ranges = ranges.TrimEnd(',');
289.             Log($"MediaPlayer_PlaybackSession_SeekableRangesChanged: {ranges}");
290.         }

292.         private async void PositionUpdateControl_PositionChanged(MediaPlaybackSession sender, object args)
293.         {
294.             var postion = sender.Position;
295.             var newSliderValue = GetSliderPositionAtPlaybackPosition(postion, m_SliderFullScale, m_PlaybackPositionMinTime, m_PlaybackPositionMaxTime);
296.             // TODO[migrate-019]: see PATTERNS.md#threading
297.             await Dispatcher.RunAsync(CoreDispatcherPriority.Normal, () =>
298.             {
299.                 CurrentPosition.Text = postion.ToString("c");
300.                 PositionSlider.Value = newSliderValue;
301.             });
302.         }

306.         private void SeekToRequestedPosition(TimeSpan requestedPosition)
307.         {
308.             Log($"Seeking to:{requestedPosition.ToString("c")}");
309.             mediaPlayerElement.MediaPlayer.PlaybackSession.Position = requestedPosition;
310.         }
311. 
312.         #endregion
313. 
314.         /// <summary>
315.         /// We will use slider ticks to drive a playback Position.
316.         ///
317.         /// This is a linear interpolation, so:
318.         /// y = m*x + b
319.         /// Where:
320.         /// Position = m*sliderPosition + b
321.         ///
322.         /// Where we have some data points:
323.         ///
324.         /// At MinPosition, we are at slider position=0
325.         /// MinPosition = m * 0 + b
326.         /// Thus:
327.         ///   b = MinPosition
328.         ///
329.         /// We choose a 100000 tick granularity for the slider,
330.         /// at which point it will be at MaxPosition:
331.         /// MaxPosition = m * 100000 + b
332.         /// MaxPosition = m * 100000 + MinPosition
333.         ///
334.         /// Thus:
335.         ///   m = (MaxPosition - MinPosition) / 100000
336.         ///   m = (MaxPosition - MinPosition) / sliderFullScale
337.         ///
338.         /// And more generally, we have:
339.         ///
340.         /// PlaybackPosition =  [(MaxPosition - MinPosition) / sliderFullScale] * SliderPosition + MinPosition
341.         /// or:
342.         /// PlaybackPosition =  ((MaxPosition - MinPosition) * SliderPosition / sliderFullScale)   + MinPosition
343.         ///
344.         /// </summary>
345.         public TimeSpan GetPlaybackPosition(double sliderPosition, double sliderFullScale, TimeSpan MinPosition, TimeSpan MaxPosition)
346.         {
347.             if (sliderFullScale == 0)
348.                 return TimeSpan.Zero;
349.             return TimeSpan.FromTicks((long)((MaxPosition - MinPosition).Ticks * sliderPosition / sliderFullScale) + MinPosition.Ticks);
350.         }
351. 
352.         /// <summary>
353.         /// Given:
354.         /// y = m * x + b
355.         /// x = (y - b) /m
356.         ///
357.         /// Where from above:
358.         ///  b = MinPosition
359.         ///  m = (MaxPosition - MinPosition) / sliderFullScale
360.         ///
361.         /// We have:
362.         ///  Position = (PlaybackPosition - MinPosition) * sliderFullScale / (MaxPosition - MinPosition)
363.         ///
364.         /// </summary>
365.         public double GetSliderPositionAtPlaybackPosition(TimeSpan playbackPosition, double sliderFullScale, TimeSpan MinPosition, TimeSpan MaxPosition)
366.         {
367.             if ((MaxPosition - MinPosition).Ticks == 0)
368.                 return 0;
369. 
370.             if (playbackPosition == TimeSpan.Zero)
371.             {
372.                 LoggerControl.Log("Transient playbackPosition == TimeSpan.Zero", LogViewLoggingLevel.Error);
373.             }
374. 
375.             var interpolatedPosition = (playbackPosition - MinPosition).Ticks * sliderFullScale / (MaxPosition - MinPosition).Ticks;
376. 
377.             if (interpolatedPosition < 0)
378.             {
379.                 interpolatedPosition = 0;
380.                 LoggerControl.Log("Transient calculatedPosition < 0", LogViewLoggingLevel.Error);
381.             }
382. 
383.             return interpolatedPosition;
384.         }

391.         private void SeekFixedAmount_Click(object sender, RoutedEventArgs e)
392.         {
393.             TimeSpan amount = TimeSpan.Zero;
394.             string selected = (sender as Button).Tag.ToString();
395.             TimeSpan.TryParse(selected, out amount);
396.             if (amount != TimeSpan.Zero)
397.             {
398.                 var requestedPosition = mediaPlayerElement.MediaPlayer.PlaybackSession.Position + amount;
399.                 SeekToRequestedPosition(requestedPosition);
400.             }
401.         }

419.         private void GoToStart_Click(object sender, RoutedEventArgs e)
420.         {
421.             if (mediaPlayerElement.MediaPlayer != null)
422.             {
423.                 var seekableRanges = mediaPlayerElement.MediaPlayer.PlaybackSession.GetSeekableRanges();
424.                 if (seekableRanges.Count > 0)
425.                 {
426.                     UpdateSeekableRangesControl(seekableRanges);
427.                     var requestedPosition = seekableRanges[0].Start;
428.                     SeekToRequestedPosition(requestedPosition);
429.                 }
430.             }
431.         }
432. 
433.         private void GoToLive_Click(object sender, RoutedEventArgs e)
434.         {
435.             if (mediaPlayerElement.MediaPlayer != null)
436.             {
437.                 var seekableRanges = mediaPlayerElement.MediaPlayer.PlaybackSession.GetSeekableRanges();
438.                 if (seekableRanges.Count > 0)
439.                 {
440.                     UpdateSeekableRangesControl(seekableRanges);
441.                     var requestedPosition = seekableRanges[seekableRanges.Count-1].End;
442.                     SeekToRequestedPosition(requestedPosition);
443.                 }
444.             }
445.         }

447.         private void RegisterForDiscreteControls(MediaPlayer mediaPlayer)
448.         {

450.             mediaPlayer.PlaybackSession.PlaybackStateChanged += MediaPlayer_PlaybackSession_PlaybackStateChanged;
451.         }

454.         {
455.             if (mediaPlayer == null)
456.             {
457.                 return;
458.             }
459. 
460.             // PlaybackSession Events
461.             mediaPlayer.PlaybackSession.PlaybackStateChanged -= MediaPlayer_PlaybackSession_PlaybackStateChanged;
462.         }
463. 
464.         private async void MediaPlayer_PlaybackSession_PlaybackStateChanged(MediaPlaybackSession sender, object args)
465.         {
466.             Log($"PlaybackSession_PlaybackStateChanged, PlaybackState: {sender.PlaybackState}");
467. 
468.             // TODO[migrate-020]: see PATTERNS.md#threading
469.             await Dispatcher.RunAsync(CoreDispatcherPriority.Normal, () =>
470.             {
471.                 switch (sender.PlaybackState)
472.                 {
473.                     case MediaPlaybackState.None:
474.                     case MediaPlaybackState.Opening:
475.                     case MediaPlaybackState.Buffering:
476.                         PlayButton.IsEnabled = false;
477.                         PauseButton.IsEnabled = false;
478.                         break;
479.                     case MediaPlaybackState.Playing:
480.                         PlayButton.IsEnabled = false;
481.                         PauseButton.IsEnabled = true;
482.                         break;
483.                     case MediaPlaybackState.Paused:
484.                         PlayButton.IsEnabled = true;
485.                         PauseButton.IsEnabled = false;
486.                         break;
487.                     default:
488.                         break;
489.                 }
490.             });
491.         }
492. 
493.         private void LogCurrentTimeCorrelation_Click(object sender, RoutedEventArgs e)
494.         {
495.             var adaptiveMediaSource = (mediaPlayerElement.MediaPlayer.Source as MediaPlaybackItem)?.Source?.AdaptiveMediaSource;
496.             if (adaptiveMediaSource == null)
497.                 return;
498.             var times = adaptiveMediaSource.GetCorrelatedTimes();
499.             Log(times.ToJsonObject().ToString());
500.         }

29.         Verbose = 0,
30.         Information,
31.         Warning,
32.         Error,

34.         Always,

37.     /// 
38.     /// <summary>
39.     /// This control can be used to log events from a scenario page.
40.     /// 
41.     /// In the XAML markup, you can set the initial logging level by setting
42.     /// LoggingLevel="Verbose", or any other enum value of LogViewLoggingLevel.
43.     /// </summary>
44.     public sealed partial class LogView : UserControl
45.     {
46.         public LogView()
47.         {
48.             this.InitializeComponent();
49. 
50.             LoggingBrushes = new SolidColorBrush[] {
51.                 (SolidColorBrush)Resources["VerboseLogBrush"],
52.                 (SolidColorBrush)Resources["InformationLogBrush"],
53.                 (SolidColorBrush)Resources["WarningLogBrush"],
54.                 (SolidColorBrush)Resources["ErrorLogBrush"],
55.                 (SolidColorBrush)Resources["CriticalLogBrush"],
56.                 (SolidColorBrush)Resources["AlwaysLogBrush"],
57.             };
58. 
59.             LogLevelComboBox.SelectedIndex = (int)LoggingLevel;
60.         }
61. 
62.         public static readonly DependencyProperty LogLevelProperty =
63.             DependencyProperty.Register("LoggingLevel", typeof(LogViewLoggingLevel), typeof(LogView),
64.                 new PropertyMetadata(LogViewLoggingLevel.Warning, OnLoggingLevelChanged));
65. 
66.         public LogViewLoggingLevel LoggingLevel
67.         {
68.             get { return (LogViewLoggingLevel)GetValue(LogLevelProperty); }
69.             set { SetValue(LogLevelProperty, value); }
70.         }
71. 
72.         private static void OnLoggingLevelChanged(DependencyObject d, DependencyPropertyChangedEventArgs e)
73.         {
74.             var logger = (LogView)d;
75.             var loggingLevel = (LogViewLoggingLevel)e.NewValue;
76.             logger.LogLevelComboBox.SelectedIndex = (int)loggingLevel;
77.         }
78. 
79.         bool IsLogViewFull = false;
80.         int MaxItemsInLogView = 500;
81. 
82.         SolidColorBrush[] LoggingBrushes;
83. 
84.         /// <summary>
85.         /// Changes the logging level at which messages are filtered.
86.         /// </summary>
87.         /// <param name="sender"></param>
88.         /// <param name="e"></param>
89.         private void LogLevel_Changed(object sender, SelectionChangedEventArgs e)
90.         {
91.             LoggingLevel = (LogViewLoggingLevel)LogLevelComboBox.SelectedIndex;
92.         }
93. 
94.         /// <summary>
95.         /// Provides logging with filtering based on UI settings.
96.         /// Logging functions tangential to main scenarios should specify a log view level less than Always.
97.         /// </summary>
98.         /// <param name="message">message to be logged</param>
99.         /// <param name="level">message category</param>
100.         public async void Log(string message, LogViewLoggingLevel level = LogViewLoggingLevel.Always)
101.         {
102.             if (Dispatcher.HasThreadAccess)
103.             {
104.                 LogFromUIThread(message, level);
105.             }
106.             else
107.             {
108.                 await Dispatcher.RunAsync(CoreDispatcherPriority.Normal, () =>
109.                 {
110.                     LogFromUIThread(message, level);
111.                 });
112.             }
113.         }
114. 
115.         private void LogFromUIThread(string message, LogViewLoggingLevel level)
116.         {
117.             string logEntry = $"{DateTime.Now:HH:mm:ss.fff} - {message}";
118. 
119.             if (LoggingLevel <= level)
120.             {
121.                 SolidColorBrush foregroundBrush = LoggingBrushes[(int)level];
122. 
123.                 if (IsLogViewFull)
124.                 {
125.                     // Once the view is full, messages go only to the debug console.
126.                     System.Diagnostics.Debug.WriteLine(logEntry);
127.                     return;
128.                 }
129. 
130.                 if (LoggingListBox.Items.Count >= MaxItemsInLogView)
131.                 {
132.                     IsLogViewFull = true;
133. 
134.                     System.Diagnostics.Debug.WriteLine(logEntry);
135.                     logEntry = "Log window is full";
136.                     foregroundBrush = new SolidColorBrush(Colors.Red);
137.                 }
138. 
139.                 LoggingListBox.Items.Insert(0, new TextBlock() { Text = logEntry, Foreground = foregroundBrush });
140.             }
141.             else if (level != LogViewLoggingLevel.Verbose)
142.             {
143.                 // Non-Verbose messages blocked from the LogView are shown on the debug console.
144.                 System.Diagnostics.Debug.WriteLine($"{level}: {logEntry}");
145.             }
146.         }
147. 
148.         private void CopyLogToClipboard_Click(object sender, RoutedEventArgs e)
149.         {
150.             var content = new DataPackage();
151.             content.SetText(String.Join("\r\n", LoggingListBox.Items.Select(item => (item as TextBlock).Text)));
152.             Clipboard.SetContent(content);
153.         }
154. 
155.         private void ClearLog_Click(object sender, RoutedEventArgs e)
156.         {
157.             ClearLog();
158.         }
159. 
160.         public async void ClearLog()
161.         {
162.             if (Dispatcher.HasThreadAccess)
163.             {
164.                 LoggingListBox.Items.Clear();
165.                 IsLogViewFull = false;
166.             }
167.             else
168.             {

<a id="threading"></a>
## Threading: CoreDispatcher → DispatcherQueueUWP (replace this):```csharp
await Dispatcher.RunAsync(CoreDispatcherPriority.Normal, () => StatusText.Text = "Done");
```WinUI 3:```csharp
DispatcherQueue.TryEnqueue(() => StatusText.Text = "Done");
DispatcherQueue.TryEnqueue(DispatcherQueuePriority.High, () => ProgressBar.Value = 100);
```Cache the queue off the UI thread via `DispatcherQueue.GetForCurrentThread()`. UWP's ASTA reentrancy protection is gone — watch for reentrancy in async code that pumps messages. See the official [threading guide](https://learn.microsoft.com/windows/apps/windows-app-sdk/migrate-to-windows-app-sdk/guides/threading).
<a id="csproj"></a>
## Project File Updates- Target a current WinAppSDK-supported TFM. The exact TFM is the source of truth in the project's `.csproj`; do not hard-code it across instruction files. Typical values at time of writing:
  - `net8.0-windows10.0.19041.0` (LTS)
  - `net9.0-windows10.0.19041.0`
  - `net10.0-windows10.0.26100.0` (current `dotnet new winui` default in this repo)
- Add `<UseWinUI>true</UseWinUI>`.
- Add `<EnableMsixTooling>true</EnableMsixTooling>` for packaged builds.
- Reference `Microsoft.WindowsAppSDK` and `Microsoft.Windows.SDK.BuildTools`.
- Reference `Microsoft.Windows.SDK.BuildTools.WinApp` to wire `dotnet run` into `winapp run`.
- Keep `Package.appxmanifest` for packaged scenarios; set `<WindowsPackageType>None</WindowsPackageType>` for unpackaged.### PackageReference reconciliation cheat-sheet`Initialize-UwpMigration.ps1` preserves the UWP `.csproj` at `<Target>/.uwp-source/` and leaves the WinUI 3 scaffold's `.csproj` intact. Open both side-by-side and merge:- **Drop** (UWP-only — never carry over):
  - `Microsoft.NETCore.UniversalWindowsPlatform`
  - Any `<PackageReference>` whose version string contains `uap` or whose name starts with `Windows.SDK.Contracts`.
- **Keep** (already in the scaffold — do not duplicate or downgrade):
  - `Microsoft.WindowsAppSDK`
  - `Microsoft.Windows.SDK.BuildTools`
- **Add** (project-specific — copy across, then update to a WinAppSDK-compatible version):
  - Third-party libraries the UWP project pulled in (`CommunityToolkit.*`, `Microsoft.Extensions.*`, `Newtonsoft.Json`, etc.). Pick the latest stable release; UWP-pinned versions are usually too old.Do **not** copy the UWP `.csproj` over the scaffold's. The two formats are incompatible — the UWP csproj carries `<TargetPlatformIdentifier>UAP</TargetPlatformIdentifier>`, `<OutputType>AppContainerExe</OutputType>`, explicit `<Compile Include="...">` items, and `Microsoft.Common.props` imports, none of which build under WinAppSDK.### Package.appxmanifest — reconcile image references with the assets you actually haveThe WinUI 3 scaffold ships with a default `Package.appxmanifest` that references assets the template provides under `Assets/` (`SplashScreen.scale-200.png`, `Square150x150Logo.scale-200.png`, `Square44x44Logo.scale-200.png`, `StoreLogo.png`, `Wide310x150Logo.scale-200.png`, `LockScreenLogo.scale-200.png`). The UWP SDK sample's manifest usually points at sample-branded assets with a `-sdk` suffix (`Splash-sdk.png`, `squareTile-sdk.png`, `SmallTile-sdk.png`, `StoreLogo-sdk.png`).If you copy the UWP manifest's `Logo` / `Square150x150Logo` / `Square44x44Logo` / `<uap:SplashScreen Image="...">` / `<uap:DefaultTile Wide310x150Logo="...">` references over the scaffold's without also bringing the matching files, **`winapp run` will register the package but the AppX layout will fail to launch** with errors like:

```
AppxManifest.xml(...): error 0x80070002: ... splash screen image [Splash-sdk.png] cannot be located.
```

(Failure code on the AppX side is typically `0x80073CF6` — "package could not be registered".)

You have two options. Pick one **before** the first `winapp run`:

- **Preferred — keep the scaffold's manifest image references** (`Assets\SplashScreen.scale-200.png`, etc.). When you me
erge any other content from the UWP manifest into the scaffold's (capabilities, file-type associations, `<uap:Extension>`
`, `<Application Id>`, etc.), do **not** overwrite the `Logo`, `<uap:SplashScreen Image>`, `Square150x150Logo`, `Square44
4x44Logo`, or `Wide310x150Logo` attribute values. The scaffold's defaults already match the files in its `Assets/` folder
r.

- **Alternative — keep the UWP sample's branded assets.** Copy every file the UWP manifest references from `.uwp-source/
/Assets/` (or `.uwp-source/<SampleName>/Assets/`) into the new project's `Assets/` folder, preserving the exact filename 
 (including the `-sdk` suffix and any scale qualifiers). Verify by re-reading each `Image=`/`Logo>` value in the manifest
t and confirming `Test-Path "Assets\<that filename>"` for every one of them. Don't rename files to look prettier — the ma
anifest reference is the source of truth.

Either way, before the first `winapp run`, sanity-check every asset reference in the manifest resolves:

```powershell
[xml]$m = Get-Content .\Package.appxmanifest
@($m.Package.Properties.Logo,
  $m.Package.Applications.Application.uap_VisualElements.Square150x150Logo,
  $m.Package.Applications.Application.uap_VisualElements.Square44x44Logo,
  $m.Package.Applications.Application.uap_VisualElements.uap_SplashScreen.Image,
  $m.Package.Applications.Application.uap_VisualElements.uap_DefaultTile.Wide310x150Logo
) | Where-Object { $_ } | ForEach-Object {
    if (-not (Test-Path $_)) { Write-Host "[MISSING] $_" -ForegroundColor Red }
}
```

### Manifest migration checklist (Windows.Desktop + runFullTrust)

The default UWP `Package.appxmanifest` declares itself as a Universal app, but a packaged WinUI 3 desktop app is a Win32
2 process with package identity — it needs a different shape. `winapp run` will register the AppX but **fail to deploy** 
 with `0x80073CF6` or "requires runFullTrust capability" if any of the following are missing.

When merging the UWP manifest into the scaffold's, make sure all of these are true:

1. **TargetDeviceFamily is `Windows.Desktop`** — not `Windows.Universal`:
   ```xml
   <Dependencies>
     <TargetDeviceFamily Name="Windows.Desktop" MinVersion="10.0.17763.0" MaxVersionTested="10.0.22621.0" />
   </Dependencies>
   ```
   Windows.Universal is UWP-only and rejected for a Win32 entrypoint.

2. **rescap namespace declared on `<Package>` and added to `IgnorableNamespaces`**:
   ```xml
   <Package
     xmlns="http://schemas.microsoft.com/appx/manifest/foundation/windows10"
     xmlns:uap="http://schemas.microsoft.com/appx/manifest/uap/windows10"
     xmlns:rescap="http://schemas.microsoft.com/appx/manifest/foundation/windows10/restrictedcapabilities"
     IgnorableNamespaces="uap rescap">
   ```
   Without the namespace declaration, item 3 below is silently stripped by AppX validation and you get the "requires run
nFullTrust" error at deploy time.

3. **`runFullTrust` capability declared**:
   ```xml
   <Capabilities>
     <rescap:Capability Name="runFullTrust" />
   </Capabilities>
   ```
   Packaged WinUI 3 desktop apps run outside the UWP AppContainer sandbox and must declare this. Keep any UWP `<Capabili
ity>` entries you actually use (e.g. `<DeviceCapability Name="webcam" />`) but add the `runFullTrust` line above no matte
er what.

4. **`<Application EntryPoint="$targetentrypoint$">`** — the WinUI 3 scaffold uses an MSBuild placeholder that the build
d resolves to the real entry point. Don't replace it with a literal `<UwpAppName>.App` (that's a UWP entry-point pattern)
).

### WUI analyzer warnings (UWP API residue)

The benchmark's `winapp build` injects the `Microsoft.WindowsAppSDK.Analyzers` package, which flags UWP-only APIs that c
compile cleanly under WinUI 3 but throw `COMException` at runtime — typically inside `Microsoft.UI.Xaml.Application.Start
t(...)` before any window can render. The runner sees this as `builds=true, runs=false`, and `Validate-UwpMigration.ps1` 
 will FAIL the build healthcheck for each unique warning.

| Rule | Symptom | Fix |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `WUI0002` | `Window.Current does not exist in WinUI 3 desktop apps` | Store the `Window` reference in `App.xaml.cs` (`
`App.Window`) and pass it where needed; see "Windowing" above. |
| `WUI0003` | `CoreDispatcher is UWP-only` | Use `DispatcherQueue.GetForCurrentThread()` and `TryEnqueue(...)`; see "Thr
reading" above. |
| `WUI0004` | `SystemNavigationManager.GetForCurrentView() is UWP-only` | Drop the system back button hookup, or use HWN
ND-based COM interop; see "GetForCurrentView Replacements" above. |

Treat every `warning WUI000\d` line in the build output as a defect — the analyzer does not produce false positives. Sea
arch for the API name in this document for the recommended replacement.

### Bootstrap-neutralized helper classes (filter-prone)

Some SDK Samples ship a shared `Common\NavigationHelper.cs` containing a `RootFrameNavigationHelper` class that wires AL
LT+Left / BrowserBack (virtual key 166) / mouse XButton1+XButton2 to frame back/forward navigation. Its WinUI 3 equivalen
nt requires `Microsoft.UI.Input.InputKeyboardSource.GetKeyState` or P/Invoke `GetAsyncKeyState` plus virtual-key code con
nstants — exactly the shape the model provider's content-safety filter classifies as "keylogger / surveillance" code. Mig
grating it routinely produces `CAPIError: 400 Output blocked by content filtering policy`.

`Initialize-UwpMigration.ps1` handles this automatically: before the agent ever sees the file, the bootstrap rewrites ju
ust the `RootFrameNavigationHelper` class body to a no-op stub (preserving the public ctor with `params object[] args` so
o callers compile no matter what signature they invoke). The audit log records `NEUTRALIZED  class RootFrameNavigationHel
lper`. The mapping row's Notes column is prefixed with `NEUTRALIZED:`.

**You don't need to migrate the neutralized class.** Just leave both it and the call site (typically `new RootFrameNavig
gationHelper(rootFrame)` in `App.xaml.cs`'s `OnLaunched`) untouched — back-nav is not a demonstrated feature of any UWP S
SDK sample. The remainder of `NavigationHelper.cs` (the `NavigationHelper` class used for per-page state save/restore, th
he LoadStateEventArgs / SaveStateEventArgs types) is migrated normally; it does not trigger the filter.

If you really want ALT+Left back-nav, add a single declarative XAML element to the AppBarButton that triggers GoBack:   

```xml
<AppBarButton Icon="Back" Click="OnBackClick">
  <AppBarButton.KeyboardAccelerators>
    <KeyboardAccelerator Key="Left" Modifiers="Menu"/>
  </AppBarButton.KeyboardAccelerators>
</AppBarButton>
```

`KeyboardAccelerator` is declarative and does not trip the filter.

544.                 // The AdaptiveMediaSource keeps track of the original PTS in an AdaptiveMediaSourceCorrelatedTimes
545.                 // object that can be retrieved via ams.GetCorrelatedTimes().  All the while, it provids the
546.                 // media pipeline with a consistent timeline that re-aligns zero at the begining  of the SeekableRange
547.                 // when first joining a Live stream.
548.                 long pts = 0;
549.                 long.TryParse(ptsTime, out pts);
550.                 var timeCorrelation = ams.GetCorrelatedTimes();
551.                 if (timeCorrelation.Position.HasValue && timeCorrelation.PresentationTimeStamp.HasValue)
552.                 {
553.                     TimeSpan currentPosition;
554.                     // TODO[migrate-013]: see PATTERNS.md#threading
555.                     await this.Dispatcher.RunAsync(CoreDispatcherPriority.Normal, () =>
556.                     {
557.                         currentPosition = mediaPlayerElement.MediaPlayer.PlaybackSession.Position;
558.                     });
559.                     TimeSpan emsgPresentationTimeDelta = TimeSpan.FromSeconds(presentation_time_delta/timescale);
560.                     long delayInTicks = timeCorrelation.PresentationTimeStamp.Value.Ticks - pts;
561.                     TimeSpan correctionForAsyncCalls = currentPosition - timeCorrelation.Position.Value;
562.                     TimeSpan targetAdPosition = emsgPresentationTimeDelta + TimeSpan.FromTicks(delayInTicks) + correctionForAsyncCalls;
563. 
564.                     Log($"Timing Info: PlaybackSession.Position:{currentPosition}  targetAdPosition:{targetAdPosition}  Delta:{targetAdPosition-currentPosition}  Ams.Position:{timeCorrelation.Position.GetValueOrDefault().Ticks}  SCTE ptsTime:{pts}  emsgPresentationTimeDelta:{emsgPresentationTimeDelta} Ams.PresentationTimeStamp:{timeCorrelation.PresentationTimeStamp.GetValueOrDefault().Ticks}  Ams.ProgramDateTime:{timeCorrelation.ProgramDateTime.GetValueOrDefault()}");
565. 
566.                     MediaBreakInsertionMethod insertionMethod = ams.IsLive ? MediaBreakInsertionMethod.Replace : MediaBreakInsertionMethod.Interrupt;
567.                     var redSkyUri = new Uri("http://az29176.vo.msecnd.net/videocontent/RedSky_FoxRiverWisc_GettyImagesRF-499617760_1080_HD_EN-US.mp4");
568.                     if (targetAdPosition != TimeSpan.Zero && targetAdPosition >= currentPosition)
569.                     {
570.                         // Schedule ad in the future:
571.                         Log($"Ad insertion triggerd by 'emsg'.  Scheduled: {targetAdPosition.ToString()} Current:{currentPosition.ToString()}");
572.                         var midRollBreak = new MediaBreak(insertionMethod, targetAdPosition);
573.                         midRollBreak.PlaybackList.Items.Add(new MediaPlaybackItem(MediaSource.CreateFromUri(redSkyUri)));
574.                         timedMetadataTrack.PlaybackItem.BreakSchedule.InsertMidrollBreak(midRollBreak);
575.                     }
576.                     else
577.                     {
578.                         // Play now!
579.                         Log($"Ad inserted immediately.  Scheduled: {targetAdPosition.ToString()} Current:{currentPosition.ToString()}");
580.                         var midRollBreak = new MediaBreak(insertionMethod);
581.                         midRollBreak.PlaybackList.Items.Add(new MediaPlaybackItem(MediaSource.CreateFromUri(redSkyUri)));
582.                         // TODO[migrate-014]: see PATTERNS.md#threading
583.                         await this.Dispatcher.RunAsync(CoreDispatcherPriority.Normal, () =>
584.                         {
585.                             mediaPlayerElement.MediaPlayer.BreakManager.PlayBreak(midRollBreak);
586.                         });
587.                     }
588.                 }
589.             }

12. using SDKTemplate.Logging.Extensions;
13. using System;
14. using Windows.Media.Streaming.Adaptive;
15. 
16. namespace SDKTemplate.Logging
17. {
18.     public class AdaptiveMediaSourceLogger : IDisposable
19.     {
20.         #region Common pattern for all loggers
21. 
22.         private LogView logView;
23.         private AdaptiveMediaSource adaptiveMediaSource;
24. 
25.         public AdaptiveMediaSourceLogger(LogView _logView, AdaptiveMediaSource _adaptiveMediaSource)
26.         {
27.             if (_logView == null)
28.                 throw new ArgumentNullException(nameof(_logView));
29.             this.logView = _logView;
30. 
31.             if (_adaptiveMediaSource == null)
32.                 throw new ArgumentNullException(nameof(_adaptiveMediaSource));
33.             this.adaptiveMediaSource = _adaptiveMediaSource;
34. 
35.             this.RegisterForAdaptiveMediaSourceEvents();
36.         }
37. 
38.         public void Dispose()
39.         {
40.             this.UnregisterForAdaptiveMediaSourceEvents();
41. 
42.             this.logView = null;
43.             this.adaptiveMediaSource = null;
44.         }
45.         
46.         #endregion
47. 
48. 
49.         #region AdaptiveMediaSource Event Handlers
50. 
51.         private void RegisterForAdaptiveMediaSourceEvents()
52.         {
53.             adaptiveMediaSource.Diagnostics.DiagnosticAvailable += DiagnosticAvailable;
54.             adaptiveMediaSource.DownloadBitrateChanged += DownloadBitrateChanged;
55.             adaptiveMediaSource.DownloadCompleted += DownloadCompleted;
56.             adaptiveMediaSource.DownloadFailed += DownloadFailed;
57.             adaptiveMediaSource.DownloadRequested += DownloadRequested;
58.             adaptiveMediaSource.PlaybackBitrateChanged += PlaybackBitrateChanged;
59.         }
60. 
61.         private void UnregisterForAdaptiveMediaSourceEvents()
62.         {
63.             if (adaptiveMediaSource == null)
64.             {
65.                 return;
66.             }
67.             adaptiveMediaSource.Diagnostics.DiagnosticAvailable -= DiagnosticAvailable;
68.             adaptiveMediaSource.DownloadBitrateChanged -= DownloadBitrateChanged;
69.             adaptiveMediaSource.DownloadCompleted -= DownloadCompleted;
70.             adaptiveMediaSource.DownloadFailed -= DownloadFailed;
71.             adaptiveMediaSource.DownloadRequested -= DownloadRequested;
72.             adaptiveMediaSource.PlaybackBitrateChanged -= PlaybackBitrateChanged;
73.         }
74. 
75.         private void DiagnosticAvailable(AdaptiveMediaSourceDiagnostics sender, AdaptiveMediaSourceDiagnosticAvailableEventArgs args)
76.         {
77.             logView.Log($"{args.ToJsonObject()}", LogViewLoggingLevel.Warning);
78.         }
79. 
80.         private void DownloadBitrateChanged(AdaptiveMediaSource sender, AdaptiveMediaSourceDownloadBitrateChangedEventArgs args)
81.         {
82.             logView.Log($"{args.ToJsonObject()}", LogViewLoggingLevel.Information);
83.         }
84. 
85.         private void DownloadCompleted(AdaptiveMediaSource sender, AdaptiveMediaSourceDownloadCompletedEventArgs args)
86.         {
87.             logView.Log($"{args.ToJsonObject()}", LogViewLoggingLevel.Verbose);
88.         }
89. 
90.         private void DownloadFailed(AdaptiveMediaSource sender, AdaptiveMediaSourceDownloadFailedEventArgs args)
91.         {
92.             logView.Log($"{args.ToJsonObject()}", LogViewLoggingLevel.Error);
93.         }
94. 
95.         private void DownloadRequested(AdaptiveMediaSource sender, AdaptiveMediaSourceDownloadRequestedEventArgs args)
96.         {
97.             logView.Log($"{args.ToJsonObject()}", LogViewLoggingLevel.Verbose);
98.         }
99. 
100.         private void PlaybackBitrateChanged(AdaptiveMediaSource sender, AdaptiveMediaSourcePlaybackBitrateChangedEventArgs args)
101.         {
102.             logView.Log($"{args.ToJsonObject()}", LogViewLoggingLevel.Information);
103.         }
104.         #endregion
105.

18. namespace SDKTemplate.Logging
19. {
20.     public class MediaPlaybackItemLogger : IDisposable
21.     {
22.         #region Common pattern for all loggers
23. 
24.         private LogView logView;
25.         private MediaPlaybackItem item;
26. 
27.         public MediaPlaybackItemLogger(LogView _logView, MediaPlaybackItem _mediaPlaybackItem)
28.         {
29.             if (_logView == null)
30.                 throw new ArgumentNullException(nameof(_logView));
31.             this.logView = _logView;
32. 
33.             if (_mediaPlaybackItem == null)
34.                 throw new ArgumentNullException(nameof(_mediaPlaybackItem));
35.             this.item = _mediaPlaybackItem;
36. 
37.             this.RegisterForMediaPlaybackItemEvents();
38.         }
39. 
40.         public void Dispose()
41.         {
42.             this.UnregisterForMediaPlaybackItemEvents();
43. 
44.             this.logView = null;
45.             this.item = null;
46.         }
47.         #endregion
48. 
49.         #region MediaPlaybackItem Event Handlers
50. 
51.         private void RegisterForMediaPlaybackItemEvents()
52.         {
53.             item.AudioTracks.SelectedIndexChanged += AudioTracks_SelectedIndexChanged;
54.             item.AudioTracksChanged += Item_AudioTracksChanged;
55.             item.VideoTracksChanged += Item_VideoTracksChanged;
56.             item.TimedMetadataTracksChanged += Item_TimedMetadataTracksChanged;
57.         }
58. 
59.         private void UnregisterForMediaPlaybackItemEvents()
60.         {
61.             if (item == null)
62.             {
63.                 return;
64.             }
65.             item.AudioTracks.SelectedIndexChanged -= AudioTracks_SelectedIndexChanged;
66.             item.AudioTracksChanged -= Item_AudioTracksChanged;
67.             item.VideoTracksChanged -= Item_VideoTracksChanged;
68.             item.TimedMetadataTracksChanged -= Item_TimedMetadataTracksChanged;
69.             foreach (AudioTrack audioTrack in item.AudioTracks)
70.             {
71.                 audioTrack.OpenFailed -= AudioTrack_OpenFailed;
72.             }
73.             foreach (VideoTrack videoTrack in item.VideoTracks)
74.             {
75.                 videoTrack.OpenFailed -= VideoTrack_OpenFailed;
76.             }
77.         }
78. 
79.         private void AudioTracks_SelectedIndexChanged(ISingleSelectMediaTrackList sender, object args)
80.         {
81.             MediaPlaybackAudioTrackList list = sender as MediaPlaybackAudioTrackList;
82.             AudioTrack audioTrack = list[sender.SelectedIndex];
83.             MediaPlaybackItem mpItem = audioTrack.PlaybackItem;
84.             object customValue;
85.             mpItem.Source.CustomProperties.TryGetValue("contentId", out customValue);
86.             string contentId = (string)customValue;
87.             string audioCodec = audioTrack.GetEncodingProperties().Subtype;
88.             var msg = $"The newly selected audio track of {contentId} has Codec {audioCodec}";
89.             var language = audioTrack.Language;
90.             if (!String.IsNullOrEmpty(language))
91.             {
92.                 // Transform the language code into a UI-localized language name.
93.                 msg += ", Language: " + (new Language(language)).DisplayName;
94.             }
95.             logView.Log(msg, LogViewLoggingLevel.Information);
96.         }
97. 
98.         private void Item_AudioTracksChanged(MediaPlaybackItem sender, IVectorChangedEventArgs args)
99.         {
100.             logView.Log($"item.AudioTracksChanged: CollectionChange:{args.CollectionChange}, Index:{args.Index}, Total:{sender.AudioTracks.Count}", LogViewLoggingLevel.Information);
101. 
102.             switch (args.CollectionChange)
103.             {
104.                 case CollectionChange.Reset:
105.                     foreach (AudioTrack track in sender.AudioTracks)
106.                     {
107.                         // Tracks are added once as a source discovers them in the source media.
108.                         // This occurs prior to the track media being opened.
109.                         // Register here so that we can receive the Track.OpenFailed event.
110.                         track.OpenFailed += AudioTrack_OpenFailed;
111.                     }
112.                     break;
113.                 case CollectionChange.ItemInserted:
114.                     // Additional tracks added after loading the main source should be registered here.
115.                     AudioTrack newTrack = sender.AudioTracks[(int)args.Index];
116.                     newTrack.OpenFailed += AudioTrack_OpenFailed;
117.                     break;
118.             }
119.         }
120. 
121.         private void Item_VideoTracksChanged(MediaPlaybackItem sender, IVectorChangedEventArgs args)
122.         {
123.             logView.Log($"item.VideoTracksChanged: CollectionChange:{args.CollectionChange}, Index:{args.Index}, Total:{sender.VideoTracks.Count}", LogViewLoggingLevel.Information);
124. 
125.             switch (args.CollectionChange)
126.             {
127.                 case CollectionChange.Reset:
128.                     foreach (VideoTrack track in sender.VideoTracks)
129.                     {
130.                         track.OpenFailed += VideoTrack_OpenFailed;
131.                     }
132.                     break;
133.                 case CollectionChange.ItemInserted:
134.                     VideoTrack newTrack = sender.VideoTracks[(int)args.Index];
135.                     newTrack.OpenFailed += VideoTrack_OpenFailed;
136.                     break;
137.             }
138.         }
139. 
140.         private void Item_TimedMetadataTracksChanged(MediaPlaybackItem sender, IVectorChangedEventArgs args)
141.         {
142.             logView.Log($"item.TimedMetadataTracksChanged: CollectionChange:{args.CollectionChange}, Index:{args.Index}, Total:{sender.TimedMetadataTracks.Count}", LogViewLoggingLevel.Information);
143. 
144.             // This is the proper time to register for timed metadata Events the app cares to consume.
145.             // See the Metadata scenario for more details.
146.         }
147. 
148.         private void AudioTrack_OpenFailed(AudioTrack sender, AudioTrackOpenFailedEventArgs args)
149.         {
150.             logView.Log($"AudioTrack.OpenFailed: ExtendedError.Message:{args.ExtendedError.Message}, ExtendedError.HResult:{args.ExtendedError.HResult.ToString("X8")}, DecoderStatus:{sender.SupportInfo.DecoderStatus}, MediaSourceStatus:{sender.SupportInfo.MediaSourceStatus}", LogViewLoggingLevel.Error);
151.         }
152. 
153.         private void VideoTrack_OpenFailed(VideoTrack sender, VideoTrackOpenFailedEventArgs args)
154.         {
155.             logView.Log($"VideoTrack.OpenFailed: ExtendedError.Message:{args.ExtendedError.Message}, ExtendedError.HResult:{args.ExtendedError.HResult.ToString("X8")}, DecoderStatus:{sender.SupportInfo.DecoderStatus}, MediaSourceStatus:{sender.SupportInfo.MediaSourceStatus}", LogViewLoggingLevel.Error);
156.         }
157.                 
158.         #endregion
159.     }

17.     public class MediaPlayerLogger : IDisposable
18.     {
19.         #region Common pattern for all loggers
20. 
21.         private LogView logView;
22.         private MediaPlayer mediaPlayer;
23. 
24.         public MediaPlayerLogger(LogView _logView, MediaPlayer _mediaPlayer)
25.         {
26.             if (_logView == null)
27.                 throw new ArgumentNullException(nameof(_logView));
28.             this.logView = _logView;
29. 
30.             if (_mediaPlayer == null)
31.                 throw new ArgumentNullException(nameof(_mediaPlayer));
32.             this.mediaPlayer = _mediaPlayer;
33. 
34.             this.RegisterForMediaPlayerEvents();
35.         }
36. 
37.         public void Dispose()
38.         {
39.             UnregisterForMediaPlayerEvents();
40. 
41.             this.logView = null;
42.             this.mediaPlayer = null;
43.         }
44. 
45.         #endregion
46. 
47.         #region MediaPlayer Event Handlers
48. 
49.         private void RegisterForMediaPlayerEvents()
50.         {
51.             // Player Events
52.             mediaPlayer.SourceChanged += MediaPlayer_SourceChanged;
53.             mediaPlayer.MediaOpened += MediaPlayer_MediaOpened;
54.             mediaPlayer.MediaEnded += MediaPlayer_MediaEnded;
55.             mediaPlayer.MediaFailed += MediaPlayer_MediaFailed;
56.             mediaPlayer.VolumeChanged += MediaPlayer_VolumeChanged;
57.             mediaPlayer.IsMutedChanged += MediaPlayer_IsMutedChanged;
58. 
59.             // NOTE: There are a number of deprecated events on MediaPlayer.
60.             // Please use the equivalent events on the MediaPlayer.PlaybackSession as shown below.
61. 
62.             // PlaybackSession Events
63.             mediaPlayer.PlaybackSession.BufferedRangesChanged += MediaPlayer_PlaybackSession_BufferedRangesChanged;
64.             mediaPlayer.PlaybackSession.BufferingEnded += MediaPlayer_PlaybackSession_BufferingEnded;
65.             mediaPlayer.PlaybackSession.BufferingProgressChanged += MediaPlayer_PlaybackSession_BufferingProgressChanged;
66.             mediaPlayer.PlaybackSession.BufferingStarted += MediaPlayer_PlaybackSession_BufferingStarted;
67.             mediaPlayer.PlaybackSession.DownloadProgressChanged += MediaPlayer_PlaybackSession_DownloadProgressChanged;
68.             mediaPlayer.PlaybackSession.NaturalDurationChanged += MediaPlayer_PlaybackSession_NaturalDurationChanged;
69.             mediaPlayer.PlaybackSession.NaturalVideoSizeChanged += MediaPlayer_PlaybackSession_NaturalVideoSizeChanged;
70.             mediaPlayer.PlaybackSession.PlaybackRateChanged += MediaPlayer_PlaybackSession_PlaybackRateChanged;
71.             mediaPlayer.PlaybackSession.PlaybackStateChanged += MediaPlayer_PlaybackSession_PlaybackStateChanged;
72.             mediaPlayer.PlaybackSession.PlayedRangesChanged += MediaPlayer_PlaybackSession_PlayedRangesChanged;
73.             mediaPlayer.PlaybackSession.PositionChanged += MediaPlayer_PlaybackSession_PositionChanged;
74.             mediaPlayer.PlaybackSession.SeekableRangesChanged += MediaPlayer_PlaybackSession_SeekableRangesChanged;
75.             mediaPlayer.PlaybackSession.SeekCompleted += MediaPlayer_PlaybackSession_SeekCompleted;
76.             mediaPlayer.PlaybackSession.SupportedPlaybackRatesChanged += MediaPlayer_PlaybackSession_SupportedPlaybackRatesChanged;

211.         private void MediaPlayer_PlaybackSession_SupportedPlaybackRatesChanged(MediaPlaybackSession sender, object args)
212.         {
213.             double minRate, maxRate;
214.             GetMinMaxRates(sender, out minRate, out maxRate);
215.             logView.Log($"MediaPlayer_PlaybackSession_SupportedPlaybackRatesChanged, MinRate: {minRate}, MaxRate: {maxRate}", LogViewLoggingLevel.Information);
216.         }
217. 
218.         private static void GetMinMaxRates(MediaPlaybackSession sender, out double minRate, out double maxRate)
219.         {
220.             minRate = -256.0;
221.             bool foundMinRate = false;
222.             for (; !foundMinRate; minRate = minRate / 2.0)
223.             {
224.                 foundMinRate = sender.IsSupportedPlaybackRateRange(minRate, 1);
225.                 if (-0.25 < minRate)
226.                 {
227.                     foundMinRate = true;
228.                     minRate = 0;
229.                 }
230.             }
231.             maxRate = 256.0;
232.             bool foundMaxRate = false;
233.             for (; !foundMaxRate; maxRate = maxRate / 2.0)
234.             {
235.                 foundMaxRate = sender.IsSupportedPlaybackRateRange(1, maxRate);
236.                 if (maxRate < 1.0)
237.                 {
238.                     foundMaxRate = true;
239.                     maxRate = 1;
240.                 }
241.             }
242.         }
243.         #endregion
244.     }
245. }

12. using SDKTemplate.Logging.Extensions;
13. using System;
14. using System.Diagnostics;

16. using System.Runtime.InteropServices.WindowsRuntime;
17. using Windows.Foundation;
18. using Windows.Web.Http;
19. using Windows.Web.Http.Filters;
20. 
21. namespace SDKTemplate.Logging
22. {
23.     public class AdaptiveMediaSourceHttpFilterLogger : IHttpFilter
24.     {
25.         #region Common pattern for all loggers
26. 
27.         private LogView logView;
28.         private IHttpFilter innerFilter;
29.         private Stopwatch timeToResponseComplete;
30. 
31.         public AdaptiveMediaSourceHttpFilterLogger(LogView _logView, IHttpFilter _innerFilter)
32.         {
33.             if (_logView == null)
34.                 throw new ArgumentNullException(nameof(_logView));
35.             this.logView = _logView;
36. 
37.             if (_innerFilter == null)
38.                 throw new ArgumentNullException(nameof(_innerFilter));
39.             this.innerFilter = _innerFilter;
40. 
41.             this.timeToResponseComplete = new Stopwatch();
42.         }
43. 
44.         public void Dispose()
45.         {
46. 
47.             this.timeToResponseComplete = null;
48.             this.logView = null;
49.             this.innerFilter = null;
50.         }
51.         
52.         #endregion
53. 
54.         public IAsyncOperationWithProgress<HttpResponseMessage, HttpProgress> SendRequestAsync(HttpRequestMessage request)
55.         {
56.             timeToResponseComplete.Start();
57.             logView.Log($"{{\"FILTER SendRequestAsync\":{{\"requestUri\":\"{request.RequestUri}\"}}}}", LogViewLoggingLevel.Verbose);
58. 
59.             return AsyncInfo.Run<HttpResponseMessage, HttpProgress>(async (cancellationToken, operationProgressReporter) =>
60.             {
61.                 IAsyncOperationWithProgress<HttpResponseMessage, HttpProgress> innerOperation = innerFilter.SendRequestAsync(request);
62. 
63.                 innerOperation.Progress += new AsyncOperationProgressHandler<HttpResponseMessage, HttpProgress>(
64.                     (IAsyncOperationWithProgress<HttpResponseMessage, HttpProgress> asyncInfo, HttpProgress httpProgressInfo) =>
65.                 {
66.                     logView.Log($"{{\"FILTER Progress Handler\": {{\"requestUri\":\"{request.RequestUri}\", {httpProgressInfo.ToJsonObject()}}}}}", LogViewLoggingLevel.Verbose);
67.                 });
68. 
69.                 HttpResponseMessage response = await innerOperation.AsTask(cancellationToken, operationProgressReporter);
70.                 cancellationToken.ThrowIfCancellationRequested();
71.                 // At this point, SendRequest has completed and we have a response.
72.                 timeToResponseComplete.Stop();
73. 
74.                 // We could inspect the content of the response headers:
75.                 var countHeaders = response.Headers.Count();
76.                 logView.Log($"{{\"FILTER Response Complete\": {{\"StatusCode\":\"{response.StatusCode}\",\"timeToResponseComplete\":\"{timeToResponseComplete.ElapsedMilliseconds}\",\"requestUri\":\"{request.RequestUri}\",\"countHeaders\":\"{countHeaders}\"}}}}", LogViewLoggingLevel.Verbose);
77. 
78.                 if(!response.IsSuccessStatusCode)
79.                 {
80.                     logView.Log($"{{\"FILTER Response Failed\": {{\"StatusCode\":\"{response.StatusCode}\",\"timeToResponseComplete\":\"{timeToResponseComplete.ElapsedMilliseconds}\",\"requestUri\":\"{request.RequestUri}\",\"countHeaders\":\"{countHeaders}\"}}}}", LogViewLoggingLevel.Error);
81.                 }
82. 
83.                 // Note that we should NOT attempt to access the response.Content in an IHttpFilter such as this,
84.                 // the Content stream is for the base filter and AdaptiveMediaSource to use.
85.                 //
86.                 // If you want to download content in the App and then pass it to the AdaptiveMediaSource, 
87.                 // see AppDownloadedKeyRequest in Scenario3, which uses Deferral objects to manage thread timing
88.                 // with the AdaptiveMediaSource.
89.                 return response;
90.             });
91.         }
92. 
93.     }
94. }

17. namespace SDKTemplate.Logging
18. {
19.     public class MediaSourceLogger : IDisposable
20.     {
21.         #region Common pattern for all loggers
22. 
23.         private LogView logView;
24.         private MediaSource source;
25. 
26.         public MediaSourceLogger(LogView _logView, MediaSource _mediaSource)
27.         {
28.             if (_logView == null)
29.                 throw new ArgumentNullException(nameof(_logView));
30.             this.logView = _logView;
31. 
32.             if (_mediaSource == null)
33.                 throw new ArgumentNullException(nameof(_mediaSource));
34.             this.source = _mediaSource;
35. 
36.             this.RegisterForMediaSourceEvents();
37.         }
38. 
39.         public void Dispose()
40.         {
41.             this.UnregisterForMediaSourceEvents();
42. 
43.             this.logView = null;
44.             this.source = null;
45.         }
46.         
47.         #endregion
48. 
49.         #region MediaSource Event Handlers
50.         private void RegisterForMediaSourceEvents()
51.         {
52.             source.StateChanged += Source_StateChanged;
53.             source.OpenOperationCompleted += Source_OpenOperationCompleted;
54.         }
55. 
56.         private void UnregisterForMediaSourceEvents()
57.         {
58.             if (source == null)
59.             {
60.                 return;
61.             }
62.             source.StateChanged -= Source_StateChanged;
63.             source.OpenOperationCompleted -= Source_OpenOperationCompleted;
64.         }
65. 
66.         private void Source_StateChanged(MediaSource sender, MediaSourceStateChangedEventArgs args)
67.         {
68.             logView.Log($"Source.StateChanged:{args.OldState} to {args.NewState}", LogViewLoggingLevel.Information);
69.         }
70. 
71.         private void Source_OpenOperationCompleted(MediaSource sender, MediaSourceOpenOperationCompletedEventArgs args)
72.         {
73.             // Get the MediaPlaybackItem that corresponds to the MediaSource.
74.             MediaPlaybackItem item = MediaPlaybackItem.FindFromMediaSource(sender);
75.             if (item != null && item.AudioTracks.Count > 0)
76.             {
77.                 // The MediaPlaybackItem contains additional information about the underlying media.
78.                 string audioCodec = item.AudioTracks[item.AudioTracks.SelectedIndex].GetEncodingProperties().Subtype;
79. 
80.                 // We can read values from the MediaSource.CustomProperties.
81.                 object customValue = null;
82.                 sender.CustomProperties.TryGetValue("uri", out customValue);
83.                 string uri = (string)customValue;
84.                 customValue = null;
85.                 sender.CustomProperties.TryGetValue("contentId", out customValue);
86.                 string contentId = (string)customValue;
87.                 logView.Log($"Opened Media Source with Uri: {uri}, ContentId: {contentId}, Codec: {audioCodec}", LogViewLoggingLevel.Information);
88. 
89.                 // This extension method in MediaPlaybackItemStringExtensions dumps all the properties from all the tracks.
90.                 var allProperties = item.ToFormattedString();
91.                 logView.Log($"MediaPlaybackItem nested properties: {allProperties}", LogViewLoggingLevel.Verbose);
92.                 // The AdaptiveMediaSource can manage multiple video tracks internally,
93.                 // but only a single video track is exposed in the MediaPlaybackItem, not a collection.
94.             }
95.         }
96.         #endregion
97.     }
98. }

23.     /// <summary>
24.     /// This class demonstrates the full range of properties available on the MediaPlaybackItem.
25.     /// 
26.     /// </summary>
27.     public static class MediaPlaybackItemStringExtensions
28.     {
29. 
30.         /// <summary>
31.         /// Provides a JSON object for Media Playback Item
32.         /// </summary>
33.         public static JsonObject ToJsonObject(this MediaPlaybackItem item)
34.         {
35.             var b = new JsonBuilder("MediaPlaybackItem");
36.             b.AddNumber("AudioTracks.Count", item.AudioTracks.Count);
37.             return b.GetJsonObject();
38.         }
39. 
40. 
41.         public static string ToFormattedString(this MediaPlaybackItem item)
42.         {
43.             if (item == null)
44.                 return String.Empty;
45. 
46.             StringBuilder sb = new StringBuilder();
47. 
48.             // MediaPlaybackItem.Source.CustomProperties
49.             var source = item.Source;
50.             if (source != null && source.CustomProperties.Any())
51.             {
52.                 sb.AppendLine();
53.                 sb.AppendLine("=-=-=-=-=-=-=-=-=-=-=-=-=-=-=-=-=-=-=");
54.                 sb.AppendLine("Source.CustomProperties:");
55.                 foreach (var prop in source.CustomProperties)
56.                 {
57.                     sb.AppendLine(prop.Key + ":\t" + prop.Value);
58.                 }
59.             }
60. 
61.             // MediaPlaybackItem.VideoTracks[].*
62.             foreach (var track in item.VideoTracks)
63.             {
64.                 // Note that AdaptiveMediaSource only exposes a single Video track.  Details on bitrates are directly on the AdaptiveMediaSource.
65.                 sb.AppendLine();
66.                 sb.AppendLine("=-=-=-=-=-=-=-=-=-=-=-=-=-=-=-=-=-=-=");
67.                 sb.AppendLine("TrackKind:\t" + track.TrackKind);
68.                 sb.AppendLine("Id:\t" + track.Id);
69.                 sb.AppendLine("Label:\t" + track.Label);
70.                 sb.AppendLine("Language:\t" + track.Language);
71.                 sb.AppendLine("Name:\t" + track.Name);
72.                 sb.AppendLine("DecoderStatus:\t" + track.SupportInfo.DecoderStatus);
73.                 sb.AppendLine("MediaSourceStatus:\t" + track.SupportInfo.MediaSourceStatus);
74.                 var encodingProps = track.GetEncodingProperties();
75.                 if (encodingProps != null)
76.                 {
77.                     sb.AppendLine("Encoding Properties:");
78.                     sb.AppendLine("Type:\t" + encodingProps.Type);
79.                     sb.AppendLine("Subtype:\t" + MediaFoundationGuidStringExtensions.ToMFAttributeName(encodingProps.Subtype));
80.                     sb.AppendLine("Bitrate:\t" + encodingProps.Bitrate);
81.                     sb.AppendLine(encodingProps.FrameRate.ToFormattedString("FrameRate"));
82.                     sb.AppendLine("Height:\t" + encodingProps.Height);
83.                     sb.AppendLine(encodingProps.PixelAspectRatio.ToFormattedString("PixelAspectRatio"));
84.                     sb.AppendLine("ProfileId:\t" + encodingProps.ProfileId);
85.                     sb.AppendLine("StereoscopicVideoPackingMode:\t" + encodingProps.StereoscopicVideoPackingMode);
86.                     sb.AppendLine("Width:\t" + encodingProps.Width);
87.                     var additionalProperties = encodingProps.Properties;
88.                     sb.AppendLine(additionalProperties.ToFormattedString());
89.                 }
90.             }
91. 
92.             // MediaPlaybackItem.AudioTracks[].*
93.             foreach (var track in item.AudioTracks)
94.             {
95.                 sb.AppendLine();
96.                 sb.AppendLine("=-=-=-=-=-=-=-=-=-=-=-=-=-=-=-=-=-=-=");
97.                 sb.AppendLine("TrackKind:\t" + track.TrackKind);
98.                 sb.AppendLine("Id:\t" + track.Id);
99.                 sb.AppendLine("Label:\t" + track.Label);
100.                 sb.AppendLine("Language:\t" + track.Language);
101.                 sb.AppendLine("Name:\t" + track.Name);
102.                 sb.AppendLine("DecoderStatus:\t" + track.SupportInfo.DecoderStatus);
103.                 sb.AppendLine("MediaSourceStatus:\t" + track.SupportInfo.MediaSourceStatus);
104.                 var encodingProps = track.GetEncodingProperties();
105.                 if (encodingProps != null)
106.                 {
107.                     sb.AppendLine("Encoding Properties:");
108.                     sb.AppendLine("Type:\t" + encodingProps.Type);
109.                     sb.AppendLine("Subtype:\t" + MediaFoundationGuidStringExtensions.ToMFAttributeName(encodingProps.Subtype));
110.                     sb.AppendLine("Bitrate:\t" + encodingProps.Bitrate);
111.                     sb.AppendLine("BitsPerSample:\t" + encodingProps.BitsPerSample);
112.                     sb.AppendLine("ChannelCount:\t" + encodingProps.ChannelCount);
113.                     sb.AppendLine("SampleRate:\t" + encodingProps.SampleRate);
114.                     var additionalProperties = encodingProps.Properties;
115.                     sb.AppendLine(additionalProperties.ToFormattedString());
116.                 }
117.             }
118. 
119.             // MediaPlaybackItem.TimedMetadataTracks[].*
120.             // Metadata tracks can be added after the open operation,
121.             // using a foreach has a risk that the collection will
122.             // change during the itteration which will throw an exception.
123.             int countOfMetadataTracks = item.TimedMetadataTracks.Count;
124.             for (int i = 0; i < countOfMetadataTracks; i++)
125.             {
126.                 var track = item.TimedMetadataTracks[i];
127. 
128.                 sb.AppendLine();
129.                 sb.AppendLine("=-=-=-=-=-=-=-=-=-=-=-=-=-=-=-=-=-=-=");
130.                 sb.AppendLine("TrackKind:\t" + track.TrackKind);
131.                 sb.AppendLine("Id:\t" + track.Id);
132.                 sb.AppendLine("Label:\t" + track.Label);
133.                 sb.AppendLine("Language:\t" + track.Language);
134.                 sb.AppendLine("Name:\t" + track.Name);
135.                 if (track.ActiveCues != null)
136.                     sb.AppendLine("ActiveCues.Count:\t" + track.ActiveCues.Count);
137.                 if (track.Cues != null)
138.                     sb.AppendLine("Cues.Count:\t" + track.Cues.Count);
139.                 sb.AppendLine("DispatchType:\t" + track.DispatchType);
140.                 sb.AppendLine("TimedMetadataKind:\t" + track.TimedMetadataKind);
141.             }
142.             return sb.ToString();
143.         }
144. 
145.         static string ToFormattedString(this MediaRatio mediaRatio, string key)
146.         {
147.             string retVal = string.Empty;
148.             if (!String.IsNullOrEmpty(key) && mediaRatio != null)
149.             {
150.                 retVal = key + ":\t" + mediaRatio.Numerator + "/" + mediaRatio.Denominator;
151.             }
152.             return retVal;
153.         }
154. 
155.         static string ToFormattedString(this MediaPropertySet mediaPropertySet)
156.         {
157.             var retVal = new StringBuilder();
158. 
159.             if (mediaPropertySet.Count != 0)
160.                 retVal.AppendLine("Additional Encoding Properties:");
161.             foreach (var prop in mediaPropertySet)
162.             {
163.                 // We can also get the various MF_ Guid-based props, some of which are a repeat from above:
164.                 retVal.AppendLine(MediaFoundationGuidStringExtensions.ToMFAttributeName(prop.Key) + ":\t" + MediaFoundationGuidStringExtensions.ToMFAttributeName(prop.Value));
165. 
166.                 // Special case for Byte Arrays:
167.                 if (prop.Value.GetType() == typeof(System.Byte[]))
168.                 {
169.                     var propBytes = prop.Value as System.Byte[];
170.                     if (propBytes != null && propBytes.Length > 0)
171.                     {
172.                         String byteString = "";
173.                         foreach (var b in propBytes)
174.                         {
175.                             byteString += String.Format("{0:X2} ", b);
176.                         }
177.                         retVal.AppendLine(MediaFoundationGuidStringExtensions.ToMFAttributeName(prop.Key) + " as Hex:\t" + byteString);
178.                     }
179.                 }
180.             }

12. using System;
13. using Windows.Data.Json;
14. 
15. namespace SDKTemplate.Logging.Extensions
16. {
17.     /// <summary>
18.     /// Provides very basic Json formatting support to the string extensions.
19.     /// </summary>
20.     public class JsonBuilder
21.     {
22.         private JsonObject container = new JsonObject();
23.         private JsonObject o = new JsonObject();
24. 
25.         public JsonBuilder(string className)
26.         {
27.             container.Add(className, o);
28.         }
29. 
30.         public void AddNumber(string key, double? value)
31.         {
32.             o.Add(key, value == null ? JsonValue.CreateNullValue() : JsonValue.CreateNumberValue(value.Value));
33.         }
34. 
35.         public void AddBoolean(string key, bool? value)
36.         {
37.             o.Add(key, value == null ? JsonValue.CreateNullValue() : JsonValue.CreateBooleanValue(value.Value));
38.         }
39. 
40.         public void AddString(string key, object value)
41.         {
42.             o.Add(key, value == null ? JsonValue.CreateNullValue() : JsonValue.CreateStringValue(value.ToString()));
43.         }
44. 
45.         public void AddTimeSpan(string key, TimeSpan? value)
46.         {
47.             o.Add(key, value == null ? JsonValue.CreateNullValue() : JsonValue.CreateStringValue(value.ToString()));
48.         }
49. 
50.         public void AddDateTime(string key, DateTimeOffset? value)
51.         {
52.             o.Add(key, value == null ? JsonValue.CreateNullValue() : JsonValue.CreateStringValue(value.Value.ToString("u")));
53.         }
54. 
55.         public void AddJsonValue(string key, IJsonValue value)
56.         {
57.             o.Add(key, value == null ? JsonValue.CreateNullValue() : value);
58.         }
59. 
60.         public JsonObject GetJsonObject()
61.         {
62.             return container;
63.         }
64. 
65.         public override string ToString()
66.         {
67.             return container.ToString();
68.         }
69.     }
70. }

13. using System;
14. using System.Collections.Generic;
15. 
16. namespace SDKTemplate.Logging.Extensions
17. {
18.     public static class MediaFoundationGuidStringExtensions
19.     {
20.         // Translation of selected GUIDs used in MF to their human-readable attribute strings
21.         // These are in defined the Windows SDK and in various regkeys.
22.         // Ref: https://msdn.microsoft.com/en-us/library/windows/desktop/dd317906(v=vs.85).aspx
23. 
24.         public static string ToMFAttributeName(Guid guidToFind)
25.         {
26.             string retVal = guidToFind.ToString();
27.             string mfAttribute;
28.             if (MfGuidDictionary.TryGetValue(guidToFind, out mfAttribute))
29.             {
30.                 retVal = mfAttribute;
31.             }
32.             return retVal;
33.         }
34.         public static string ToMFAttributeName(object objectToCheck)
35.         {
36.             // Return .ToString as default:
37.             string retVal = objectToCheck.ToString();
38. 
39.             // Passed object is Guid
40.             if (objectToCheck is Guid)
41.             {
42.                 Guid guidToFind = (Guid)objectToCheck;
43.                 retVal = ToMFAttributeName(guidToFind);
44.             }
45.             else
46.             {
47.                 // Passed object is Guid.ToString()
48.                 Guid guidToFind;
49.                 if (Guid.TryParse(retVal, out guidToFind))
50.                 {
51.                     retVal = ToMFAttributeName(guidToFind);
52.                 }
53.             }
54.             return retVal;
55.         }
56. 
57.

59.         {

49.         private KeyValuePair<string, object> MediaProtectionSystemId = new KeyValuePair<string, object>("Windows.Media.Protection.MediaProtectionSystemId", "{F4637010-03C3-42CD-B932-B48ADF3A6A54}");
50.         private KeyValuePair<string, object> MediaProtectionContainerGuid = new KeyValuePair<string, object>("Windows.Media.Protection.MediaProtectionContainerGuid", "{9A04F079-9840-4286-AB92-E65BE0885F95}");

55.         public string playReadyChallengeCustomData = "";

58.         private void Log(string message)
59.         {
60.             if (logger != null)
61.             {
62.                 logger.Log(message, LogViewLoggingLevel.Information);
63.             }
64.             else
65.             {
66.                 string logEntry = $"{DateTime.Now:HH:mm:ss.fff} - {message}";
67.                 System.Diagnostics.Debug.WriteLine(logEntry);
68.             }
69.         }
