71.         public void SetUpProtectionManager(MediaPlayer mediaPlayer)
72.         {
73.             Log("Enter SetUpProtectionManager");
74. 
75.             if(mediaPlayer == null)
76.                 throw new ArgumentException("SetUpProtectionManager was passed a null MediaPlayer");
77.             
78.             Log("Creating protection system mappings...");
79.             var protectionManager = new MediaProtectionManager();
80. 
81.             protectionManager.ComponentLoadFailed += new ComponentLoadFailedEventHandler(ProtectionManager_ComponentLoadFailed);
82.             protectionManager.ServiceRequested += new ServiceRequestedEventHandler(ProtectionManager_ServiceRequested);
83. 
84.             // The code here is mandatory and should be just copied directly over to the app
85.             // Ref: https://msdn.microsoft.com/en-us/library/windows/desktop/aa376846%28v=vs.85%29.aspx
86. 
87.             // Setup PlayReady as the ProtectionSystem to use by mediaFoundation: 
88.             var contentProtectionSystems = new PropertySet();
89.             contentProtectionSystems.Add(PlayReadyWinRTTrustedInput);
90.             protectionManager.Properties.Add(MediaProtectionSystemIdMapping, contentProtectionSystems);
91.             protectionManager.Properties.Add(MediaProtectionSystemId);
92.             protectionManager.Properties.Add(MediaProtectionContainerGuid);
93. 
94.             mediaPlayer.ProtectionManager = protectionManager;
95. 
96.             Log("Leave SetUpProtectionManager");
97.         }
98. 
99.         private async void ProtectionManager_ServiceRequested(MediaProtectionManager sender, ServiceRequestedEventArgs e)
100.         {
101.             Log("Enter ProtectionManager_ServiceRequested");
102. 
103.             if (e.Request is PlayReadyIndividualizationServiceRequest)
104.             {
105.                 var individualizationRequest = e.Request as PlayReadyIndividualizationServiceRequest;
106.                 bool bResultIndiv = await ReactiveIndividualizationRequestAsync(individualizationRequest, e.Completion);
107.             }
108.             else if (e.Request is PlayReadyLicenseAcquisitionServiceRequest)
109.             {
110.                 var licenseRequest = e.Request as PlayReadyLicenseAcquisitionServiceRequest;
111.                 LicenseAcquisitionRequest(licenseRequest, e.Completion, playReadyLicenseUrl, playReadyChallengeCustomData);
112.             }
113. 
114.             Log("Leave ProtectionManager_ServiceRequested");
115.         }
116. 
117.         private void ProtectionManager_ComponentLoadFailed(MediaProtectionManager sender, ComponentLoadFailedEventArgs e)
118.         {
119.             Log("Enter ProtectionManager_ComponentLoadFailed");
120.             Log(e.Information.ToString());
121. 
122.             // List the failing components - RevocationAndRenewalInformation
123.             foreach (var item in e.Information.Items)
124.             {
125.                 Log(item.Name + 
126.                     "\nReasons=0x" + item.Reasons + 
127.                     "\nRenewal Id=" + item.RenewalId);
128.             }
129.             e.Completion.Complete(false);
130. 
131.             Log("Leave ProtectionManager_ComponentLoadFailed");
132.         }
133. 
134.         /// <summary>
135.         /// Invoked to acquire the PlayReady License
136.         /// </summary>
137.         async void LicenseAcquisitionRequest(PlayReadyLicenseAcquisitionServiceRequest licenseRequest, MediaProtectionServiceCompletion completionNotifier, string url, string challengeCustomData)
138.         {
139.             bool bResult = false;
140.             string ExceptionMessage = string.Empty;
141. 
142.             try
143.             {
144.                 if (!string.IsNullOrEmpty(url))
145.                 {
146.                     Log("ProtectionManager PlayReady Manual License Acquisition Service Request in progress - URL: " + url);
147. 
148.                     if (!string.IsNullOrEmpty(challengeCustomData))
149.                     {
150.                         var encoding = new UTF8Encoding();
151.                         byte[] b = encoding.GetBytes(challengeCustomData);
152.                         licenseRequest.ChallengeCustomData = Convert.ToBase64String(b, 0, b.Length);
153.                     }
154. 
155.                     PlayReadySoapMessage soapMessage = licenseRequest.GenerateManualEnablingChallenge();
156. 
157.                     byte[] messageBytes = soapMessage.GetMessageBody();
158.                     HttpContent httpContent = new ByteArrayContent(messageBytes);
159. 
160.                     IPropertySet propertySetHeaders = soapMessage.MessageHeaders;
161.                     foreach (string strHeaderName in propertySetHeaders.Keys)
162.                     {
163.                         string strHeaderValue = propertySetHeaders[strHeaderName].ToString();
164. 
165.                         // The Add method throws an ArgumentException try to set protected headers like "Content-Type"
166.                         // so set it via "ContentType" property
167.                         if (strHeaderName.Equals("Content-Type", StringComparison.OrdinalIgnoreCase))
168.                             httpContent.Headers.ContentType = MediaTypeHeaderValue.Parse(strHeaderValue);
169.                         else
170.                             httpContent.Headers.Add(strHeaderName.ToString(), strHeaderValue);
171.                     }
172.                     var licenseAcquision = new CommonLicenseRequest();
173.                     HttpContent responseHttpContent = await licenseAcquision.AcquireLicenseAsync(new Uri(url), httpContent);
174.                     if (responseHttpContent != null)
175.                     {
176.                         Exception exResult = licenseRequest.ProcessManualEnablingResponse(await responseHttpContent.ReadAsByteArrayAsync());
177.                         if (exResult != null)
178.                         {
179.                             throw exResult;
180.                         }
181.                         bResult = true;
182.                     }
183.                     else
184.                         ExceptionMessage = licenseAcquision.GetLastErrorMessage();
185.                 }
186.                 else
187.                 {
188.                     Log("ProtectionManager PlayReady License Acquisition Service Request in progress - URL: " + licenseRequest.Uri.ToString());
189.                     await licenseRequest.BeginServiceRequest();
190.                     bResult = true;
191.                 }
192.             }
193.             catch (Exception e)
194.             {
195.                 ExceptionMessage = e.Message;
196.             }
197. 
198.             if (bResult == true)
199.                 Log(!string.IsNullOrEmpty(url) ? "ProtectionManager Manual PlayReady License Acquisition Service Request successful" :
200.                     "ProtectionManager PlayReady License Acquisition Service Request successful");
201.             else
202.                 Log(!string.IsNullOrEmpty(url) ? "ProtectionManager Manual PlayReady License Acquisition Service Request failed: " + ExceptionMessage :
203.                     "ProtectionManager PlayReady License Acquisition Service Request failed: " + ExceptionMessage);
204.             completionNotifier.Complete(bResult);
205.         }
206.         /// <summary>
207.         /// Proactive Individualization Request 
208.         /// </summary>
209.         async void ProactiveIndividualizationRequest()
210.         {
211.             var individualizationRequest = new PlayReadyIndividualizationServiceRequest();
212.             Log("ProtectionManager PlayReady ProActive Individualization Service Request in progress...");
213.             bool bResultIndiv = await ReactiveIndividualizationRequestAsync(individualizationRequest, null);
214.             if (bResultIndiv == true)
215.                 Log("ProtectionManager PlayReady ProActive Individualization Service Request successful");
216.             else
217.                 Log("ProtectionManager PlayReady ProActive Individualization Service Request failed");
218. 
219.         }

49. 
50.     public class BitrateHelper
51.     {
52.         // Each bitrate is assigned a symbol as follows:
53.         // The bottom quartile gets 1 bar, the second quartile gets 2 bars,
54.         // the third quartile gets 3 bars, and the top quartile gets 4 bars.
55.         private Dictionary<uint, Symbol> symbolAssignment = new Dictionary<uint, Symbol>();
56. 
57.         public BitrateHelper(IEnumerable<uint> availableBitRates)
58.         {
59.             uint[] sortedBitRates = availableBitRates.ToArray();
60.             Array.Sort(sortedBitRates);
61.             int i = 0;
62.             while (i < sortedBitRates.Length * 1 / 4)
63.             {
64.                 symbolAssignment.Add(sortedBitRates[i++], Symbol.OneBar);
65.             }
66.             while (i < sortedBitRates.Length * 2 / 4)
67.             {
68.                 symbolAssignment.Add(sortedBitRates[i++], Symbol.TwoBars);
69.             }
70.             while (i < sortedBitRates.Length * 3 / 4)
71.             {
72.                 symbolAssignment.Add(sortedBitRates[i++], Symbol.ThreeBars);
73.             }
74.             while (i < sortedBitRates.Length * 4 / 4)
75.             {
76.                 symbolAssignment.Add(sortedBitRates[i++], Symbol.FourBars);
77.             }
78.         }
79. 
80.         public Symbol GetBitrateSymbol(uint currentBitrate)
81.         {
82.             Symbol symbol = Symbol.ZeroBars;
83.             if (symbolAssignment.TryGetValue(currentBitrate, out symbol))
84.             {
85.                 return symbol;
86.             }
87.             return Symbol.ZeroBars;
88.         }
89.     }
90. }

228.             // On small screens, hide the description text to make room for the video.

237.         #region AdaptiveMediaSource Event Handlers
238. 
239.         private void RegisterForAdaptiveMediaSourceEvents(AdaptiveMediaSource adaptiveMediaSource)
240.         {
241.             adaptiveMediaSource.DownloadRequested += DownloadRequested;
242.             adaptiveMediaSource.DownloadFailed += DownloadFailed;
243.         }
244. 
245.         private void UnregisterForAdaptiveMediaSourceEvents(AdaptiveMediaSource adaptiveMediaSource)
246.         {
247.             if (adaptiveMediaSource != null)
248.             {
249.                 adaptiveMediaSource.DownloadRequested -= DownloadRequested;
250.                 adaptiveMediaSource.DownloadFailed -= DownloadFailed;
251.             }
252.         }
253. 
254.         private async void DownloadRequested(AdaptiveMediaSource sender, AdaptiveMediaSourceDownloadRequestedEventArgs args)
255.         {
256.             if (args.ResourceType == AdaptiveMediaSourceResourceType.Key)
257.             {
258.                 switch (tokenMethod)
259.                 {
260.                     case AzureKeyAcquisitionMethod.None:
261.                         break;
262.                     case AzureKeyAcquisitionMethod.AuthorizationHeader:
263.                         // By updating the IHttpFilter KeyHost property here, we ensure it's up to date
264.                         // before the network request is made.  It is the IHttpFilter that will insert
265.                         // the Authorization header.
266.                         if (httpClientFilter != null)
267.                         {
268.                             httpClientFilter.KeyHost = args.ResourceUri.Host;
269.                         }
270.                         break;
271.                     case AzureKeyAcquisitionMethod.UrlQueryParameter:
272.                         ModifyKeyRequestUri(args);
273.                         break;
274.                     case AzureKeyAcquisitionMethod.ApplicationDownloaded:
275.                         await AppDownloadedKeyRequest(args);
276.                         break;
277.                     default:
278.                         break;
279.                 }
280.             }
281.         }
282. 
283.         private void ModifyKeyRequestUri(AdaptiveMediaSourceDownloadRequestedEventArgs args)
284.         {
285.             if (ContentSelectorControl.SelectedModel == null)
286.             {
287.                 return;
288.             }
289. 
290.             // This pattern can be used to modify Uris, for example:
291.             //   To a redirect traffic to a secondary endpoint
292.             //   To change an segment request into a byte-range Uri into another resource
293. 
294.             // Add the Bearer token to the Uri and modify the args.Result.ResourceUri
295.             string armoredAuthToken = System.Net.WebUtility.UrlEncode("Bearer=" + ContentSelectorControl.SelectedModel.AesToken);
296.             string uriWithTokenParameter = $"{args.ResourceUri.AbsoluteUri}&token={armoredAuthToken}";
297.             args.Result.ResourceUri = new Uri(uriWithTokenParameter);
298.         }
299. 
300.         private async Task AppDownloadedKeyRequest(AdaptiveMediaSourceDownloadRequestedEventArgs args)
301.         {
302.             if (ContentSelectorControl.SelectedModel == null)
303.             {
304.                 return;
305.             }
306. 
307.             // For AzureKeyAcquisitionMethod.ApplicationDownloaded we do the following:
308.             //   Call .GetDeferral() to allow asynchronous work to be performed
309.             //   Get the requested network resource using app code
310.             //   Set .Result.InputStream or .Result.Buffer with the response data
311.             //   Complete the deferral to indicate that we are done.
312.             // With this pattern, the app has complete control over any downloaded content.
313. 
314.             // Obtain a deferral so we can perform asynchronous operations.
315.             var deferral = args.GetDeferral();
316.             try
317.             {
318.                 var appHttpClientForKeys = new HttpClient();
319.                 appHttpClientForKeys.DefaultRequestHeaders.Authorization = new HttpCredentialsHeaderValue("Bearer", ContentSelectorControl.SelectedModel.AesToken);
320. 
321.                 HttpRequestMessage request = new HttpRequestMessage(HttpMethod.Get, args.ResourceUri);
322. 
323.                 HttpResponseMessage response = await appHttpClientForKeys.SendRequestAsync(
324.                     request, HttpCompletionOption.ResponseHeadersRead).AsTask(ctsForAppHttpClientForKeys.Token);
325. 
326.                 if (response.IsSuccessStatusCode)
327.                 {
328.                     args.Result.InputStream = await response.Content.ReadAsInputStreamAsync();
329.                     // Alternatively, we could use:
330.                     // args.Result.Buffer = await response.Content.ReadAsBufferAsync();
331.                 }
332.                 else
333.                 {
334.                     // The app code failed. Report this by setting the args.Result.ExtendedStatus.
335.                     // This will ensure that the AdaptiveMediaSource does not attempt to download the resource
336.                     // itself, and instead treats the download as having failed.
337.                     switch (response.StatusCode)
338.                     {
339.                         case HttpStatusCode.Unauthorized:
340.                             // HTTP_E_STATUS_DENIED
341.                             args.Result.ExtendedStatus = 0x80190191;
342.                             break;
343.                         case HttpStatusCode.NotFound:
344.                             // HTTP_E_STATUS_NOT_FOUND
345.                             args.Result.ExtendedStatus = 0x80190194;
346.                             break;
347.                         default:
348.                             // HTTP_E_STATUS_UNEXPECTED
349.                             args.Result.ExtendedStatus = 0x80190001;
350.                             break;
351.                     }
352.                     Log($"Key Download Failed: {response.StatusCode} {args.ResourceUri}");
353.                 }
354.             }
355.             catch (TaskCanceledException)
356.             {
357.                 Log($"Request canceled: {args.ResourceUri}");
358. 
359.             }
360.             catch (Exception e)
361.             {
362.                 Log($"Key Download Failed: {e.Message} {args.ResourceUri}");
363.             }
364.             finally
365.             {
366.                 // Complete the deferral when done.
367.                 deferral.Complete();
368.             }
369.         }
370. 
371.         private void DownloadFailed(AdaptiveMediaSource sender, AdaptiveMediaSourceDownloadFailedEventArgs args)
372.         {
373.             Log($"DownloadFailed: {args.HttpResponseMessage}, {args.ResourceType}, {args.ResourceUri}");
374.         }

402.         /// <summary>
403.         /// Handles the radio button selections from the UI and imposes that minimum desired protection
404.         /// </summary>
405.         private async void HdcpDesiredMinimumProtection_Click(object sender, RoutedEventArgs e)
406.         {
407.             HdcpProtection desiredMinimumProtection = HdcpProtection.Off;
408.             string selected = (sender as RadioButton).Tag.ToString();
409.             if (Enum.TryParse(selected, out desiredMinimumProtection))
410.             {
411.                 var result = await hdcpSession.SetDesiredMinProtectionAsync(desiredMinimumProtection);
412.                 HdcpProtection? actualProtection = hdcpSession.GetEffectiveProtection();
413.                 if (result != HdcpSetProtectionResult.Success)
414.                 {
415.                     Log($"ERROR: Unable to set HdcpProtection.{desiredMinimumProtection}, Error: {result}, Actual HDCP: {actualProtection}");
416.                 }
417.                 else
418.                 {
419.                     Log($"HDCP Requested Minimum: {desiredMinimumProtection}, Actual: {actualProtection}");
420.                 }
421.             }
422.         }

447.                             DesiredMaxBitrateText.Text = "full bitrate allowed";
448.                             break;
449.                         case HdcpProtection.On:
450.                             // When there is no HDCP Type 1, make the highest bitrate unavailable.

<a id="startup-crashes"></a>
## Diagnosing Startup CrashesA packaged WinUI 3 app can build cleanly and still crash the instant it launches — `winapp run` returns no usable window and the process is gone. The build log and the analyzer cannot see this; only a real launch does. The **wrong** way to chase it is to sprinkle `File.WriteAllText` traces through `Program.cs` / `App.xaml.cs` and re-run repeatedly — that burns time and usually misses the real frame (the crash often happens in native activation, before your managed `Main` even runs).**Capture the real exception first.** Either run the launch-and-diagnose helper:```powershell
& "<skill-root>/scripts/Test-AppLaunch.ps1" -Target "<winui3-project-root>"
```…or read the Windows **Application** event log directly for the two events the crash writes:```powershell
Get-WinEvent -LogName Application -MaxEvents 40 |
  Where-Object { $_.Id -in 1000,1026 -and $_.TimeCreated -gt (Get-Date).AddMinutes(-3) } |
  ForEach-Object { "`n=== Event $($_.Id) @ $($_.TimeCreated) ==="; $_.Message }
```- **Event 1000** — faulting module + native **Exception code** (e.g. `0x8001010E`).
- **Event 1026** — *.NET Runtime*: the managed **exception type, message, and stack**. This is the one that names the actual throwing frame. Read it before changing any code.**Known native codes and what they usually mean** (the code names the error *class*; the real fix is whatever the captured stack points at):| Exception code | Meaning | Usual migration cause → where to look |
|---|---|---|
| `0x80004003` | `E_POINTER` | Static-window **init-order race** — a `Page` read `App.MainWindow` (or another static window reference) before `OnLaunched` assigned it. Keep `MainWindow`'s constructor inert and navigate after `Activate`. See [Initialization order](#windowing). |
| `0x8001010E` | `RPC_E_WRONG_THREAD` | A **thread/apartment-affined object** was accessed during startup — commonly a view- or `CoreWindow`-affined UWP API touched from a `static` initializer, a type constructor, or off the UI thread. Construct/access it on the UI thread *after* `Activate`. If the API has no WinUI 3 desktop equivalent, defer it. |
| `0xE0434352` | Managed CLR exception | Read the **.NET exception type** in event 1026. `TypeLoadException` / `FileNotFoundException` almost always means a missing or version-incompatible package reference, not your code. |
| `0xC000027B` | Native stowed exception | Often a legacy projection/activation incompatibility for an API used at startup. If the API/contract is unsupported on the current OS, defer it. |> Do **not** assume the entry point is the problem. A custom `Program.Main` for WinUI 3 **correctly** carries `[STAThread]` + `ComWrappersSupport.InitializeComWrappers()` + the `DispatcherQueueSynchronizationContext` setup — this matches the SDK's auto-generated `Main`. `[STAThread]` is **required**, not a bug. If you have a hand-written entry point and don't need single-instancing/redirection, the simplest path is to delete it and let the SDK generate `Main`.

The Step 4 validator runs this same check (`Validate-UwpMigration.ps1` Section 7) and **fails** when the app registers but dies at startup, surfacing the captured signature in `.validator-diagnostics.txt`.
